        Idea:
Persistent tech debt registry for megaplan

## Problem
The same subsystem gets flagged and point-fixed across multiple plans without being properly redesigned. In this session, timeout recovery was flagged in 4 separate plans. Each plan did a point fix. The critiquer had no way to know 'this has been flagged before — stop patching and redesign it.'

## Solution
A .megaplan/debt.json registry that persists across plans:

1. When a critique flag is accepted as a known limitation (force-proceed, or gate says 'accepted tradeoff'), it gets added to debt.json with the flag ID, concern, which plan accepted it, and when.

2. The critique prompt includes the debt registry as context: 'These are known accepted limitations — don't re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse.'

3. The gate prompt includes it too: if the current plan's flags overlap with existing debt, the gate can note 'this is recurring debt, consider addressing it holistically.'

4. The executor prompt includes relevant debt items as watch items: 'Don't make these worse.'

5. A CLI command to manage debt: megaplan debt list, megaplan debt add, megaplan debt resolve. Resolving removes it and records which plan fixed it.

6. When debt items accumulate in the same subsystem (e.g. 3+ items tagged 'timeout-recovery'), surface this clustering to the critiquer as a signal to recommend redesign rather than another point fix.

User notes and answers:
- Critical design requirement: debt entries must be structured and deduplicated. Each entry needs: a subsystem tag (e.g. 'timeout-recovery', 'execute-paths', 'observation'), a normalized concern string, and the flag IDs that contributed to it. Before adding, the system must check existing entries by subsystem tag and semantic similarity of the concern — if a match exists, increment its occurrence count and append the new flag ID rather than creating a duplicate. The critique prompt should show debt entries grouped by subsystem so the critiquer sees clustering naturally. The gate should auto-escalate when a subsystem has 3+ debt entries: 'This subsystem has been flagged N times across M plans — recommend holistic redesign rather than another point fix.' The add operation should be available both as a CLI command (megaplan debt add) and as an automatic side-effect when a gate force-proceeds past a flag.

        Idea:
Persistent tech debt registry for megaplan

## Problem
The same subsystem gets flagged and point-fixed across multiple plans without being properly redesigned. In this session, timeout recovery was flagged in 4 separate plans. Each plan did a point fix. The critiquer had no way to know 'this has been flagged before — stop patching and redesign it.'

## Solution
A .megaplan/debt.json registry that persists across plans:

1. When a critique flag is accepted as a known limitation (force-proceed, or gate says 'accepted tradeoff'), it gets added to debt.json with the flag ID, concern, which plan accepted it, and when.

2. The critique prompt includes the debt registry as context: 'These are known accepted limitations — don't re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse.'

3. The gate prompt includes it too: if the current plan's flags overlap with existing debt, the gate can note 'this is recurring debt, consider addressing it holistically.'

4. The executor prompt includes relevant debt items as watch items: 'Don't make these worse.'

5. A CLI command to manage debt: megaplan debt list, megaplan debt add, megaplan debt resolve. Resolving removes it and records which plan fixed it.

6. When debt items accumulate in the same subsystem (e.g. 3+ items tagged 'timeout-recovery'), surface this clustering to the critiquer as a signal to recommend redesign rather than another point fix.

User notes and answers:
- Critical design requirement: debt entries must be structured and deduplicated. Each entry needs: a subsystem tag (e.g. 'timeout-recovery', 'execute-paths', 'observation'), a normalized concern string, and the flag IDs that contributed to it. Before adding, the system must check existing entries by subsystem tag and semantic similarity of the concern — if a match exists, increment its occurrence count and append the new flag ID rather than creating a duplicate. The critique prompt should show debt entries grouped by subsystem so the critiquer sees clustering naturally. The gate should auto-escalate when a subsystem has 3+ debt entries: 'This subsystem has been flagged N times across M plans — recommend holistic redesign rather than another point fix.' The add operation should be available both as a CLI command (megaplan debt add) and as an automatic side-effect when a gate force-proceeds past a flag.

Missing one will be a runtime TypeError. They can differ. All debt I/O uses root. Double-check every load/save call.\n\n**Backward compatibility**: debt.json won't exist for plans started before this feature. {
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/_core.py\n@@\n from megaplan.types import (\n     CliError,\n+    DEBT_ESCALATION_THRESHOLD,\n+    DebtEntry,\n+    DebtRegistry,\n     FLAG_BLOCKING_STATUSES,\n     FlagRecord,\n     FlagRegistry,\n@@\n def save_flag_registry(plan_dir: Path, registry: FlagRegistry) -> None:\n     atomic_write_json(plan_dir / \"faults.json\", registry)\n \n \n+def load_debt_registry(root: Path) -> DebtRegistry:\n+    path = megaplan_root(root) / \"debt.json\"\n+    if path.exists():\n+        return read_json(path)\n+    return {\"entries\": []}\n+\n+\n+def save_debt_registry(root: Path, registry: DebtRegistry) -> None:\n+    atomic_write_json(megaplan_root(root) / \"debt.json\", registry)\n+\n+\n+def next_debt_id(registry: DebtRegistry) -> str:\n+    max_id = 0\n+    for entry in registry[\"entries\"]:\n+        match = re.fullmatch(r\"DEBT-(\\d+)\", entry[\"id\"])\n+        if match is None:\n+            continue\n+        max_id = max(max_id, int(match.group(1)))\n+    return f\"DEBT-{max_id + 1:03d}\"\n+\n+\n+def _normalize_subsystem_tag(value: str) -> str:\n+    normalized = re.sub(r\"[^a-z0-9]+\", \"-\", value.lower()).strip(\"-\")\n+    return normalized or \"untagged\"\n+\n+\n+def extract_subsystem_tag(concern: str) -> str:\n+    prefix, separator, _ = concern.partition(\":\")\n+    if not separator:\n+        return \"untagged\"\n+    return _normalize_subsystem_tag(prefix)\n+\n+\n+def _concern_word_set(concern: str) -> set[str]:\n+    return {\n+        token\n+        for token in re.split(r\"[^a-z0-9]+\", normalize_text(concern))\n+        if token\n+    }\n+\n+\n+def _jaccard_similarity(left: set[str], right: set[str]) -> float:\n+    if not left or not right:\n+        return 0.0\n+    union = left | right\n+    if not union:\n+        return 0.0\n+    return len(left & right) / len(union)\n+\n+\n+def find_matching_debt(registry: DebtRegistry, subsystem: str, concern: str) -> DebtEntry | None:\n+    normalized_subsystem = _normalize_subsystem_tag(subsystem)\n+    concern_words = _concern_word_set(concern)\n+    for entry in registry[\"entries\"]:\n+        if entry[\"resolved\"]:\n+            continue\n+        if entry[\"subsystem\"] != normalized_subsystem:\n+            continue\n+        if _jaccard_similarity(_concern_word_set(entry[\"concern\"]), concern_words) > 0.5:\n+            return entry\n+    return None\n+\n+\n+def add_or_increment_debt(\n+    registry: DebtRegistry,\n+    subsystem: str,\n+    concern: str,\n+    flag_ids: list[str],\n+    plan_id: str,\n+) -> DebtEntry:\n+    normalized_subsystem = _normalize_subsystem_tag(subsystem)\n+    normalized_concern = normalize_text(concern)\n+    timestamp = now_utc()\n+    existing = find_matching_debt(registry, normalized_subsystem, normalized_concern)\n+    if existing is not None:\n+        existing[\"occurrence_count\"] += 1\n+        existing[\"updated_at\"] = timestamp\n+        for flag_id in flag_ids:\n+            if flag_id not in existing[\"flag_ids\"]:\n+                existing[\"flag_ids\"].append(flag_id)\n+        if plan_id not in existing[\"plan_ids\"]:\n+            existing[\"plan_ids\"].append(plan_id)\n+        return existing\n+\n+    entry: DebtEntry = {\n+        \"id\": next_debt_id(registry),\n+        \"subsystem\": normalized_subsystem,\n+        \"concern\": normalized_concern,\n+        \"flag_ids\": list(dict.fromkeys(flag_ids)),\n+        \"plan_ids\": [plan_id],\n+        \"occurrence_count\": 1,\n+        \"created_at\": timestamp,\n+        \"updated_at\": timestamp,\n+        \"resolved\": False,\n+        \"resolved_by\": None,\n+        \"resolved_at\": None,\n+    }\n+    registry[\"entries\"].append(entry)\n+    return entry\n+\n+\n+def resolve_debt(registry: DebtRegistry, debt_id: str, plan_id: str) -> DebtEntry:\n+    for entry in registry[\"entries\"]:\n+        if entry[\"id\"] != debt_id:\n+            continue\n+        timestamp = now_utc()\n+        entry[\"resolved\"] = True\n+        entry[\"resolved_by\"] = plan_id\n+        entry[\"resolved_at\"] = timestamp\n+        entry[\"updated_at\"] = timestamp\n+        return entry\n+    raise CliError(\"missing_debt\", f\"Debt entry '{debt_id}' does not exist\")\n+\n+\n+def debt_by_subsystem(registry: DebtRegistry) -> dict[str, list[DebtEntry]]:\n+    grouped: dict[str, list[DebtEntry]] = {}\n+    for entry in registry[\"entries\"]:\n+        if entry[\"resolved\"]:\n+            continue\n+        grouped.setdefault(entry[\"subsystem\"], []).append(entry)\n+    return grouped\n+\n+\n+def subsystem_occurrence_total(entries: list[DebtEntry]) -> int:\n+    return sum(entry[\"occurrence_count\"] for entry in entries)\n+\n+\n+def escalated_subsystems(registry: DebtRegistry) -> list[tuple[str, int, list[DebtEntry]]]:\n+    escalated: list[tuple[str, int, list[DebtEntry]]] = []\n+    for subsystem, entries in debt_by_subsystem(registry).items():\n+        total = subsystem_occurrence_total(entries)\n+        if total >= DEBT_ESCALATION_THRESHOLD:\n+            escalated.append((subsystem, total, entries))\n+    escalated.sort(key=lambda item: (-item[1], item[0]))\n+    return escalated\n+\n+\n def unresolved_significant_flags(flag_registry: FlagRegistry) -> list[FlagRecord]:\n     return [\n         flag\n         for flag in flag_registry[\"flags\"]\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Idea:
Persistent tech debt registry for megaplan

## Problem
The same subsystem gets flagged and point-fixed across multiple plans without being properly redesigned. In this session, timeout recovery was flagged in 4 separate plans. Each plan did a point fix. The critiquer had no way to know 'this has been flagged before — stop patching and redesign it.'

## Solution
A .megaplan/debt.json registry that persists across plans:

1. When a critique flag is accepted as a known limitation (force-proceed, or gate says 'accepted tradeoff'), it gets added to debt.json with the flag ID, concern, which plan accepted it, and when.

2. The critique prompt includes the debt registry as context: 'These are known accepted limitations — don't re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse.'

3. The gate prompt includes it too: if the current plan's flags overlap with existing debt, the gate can note 'this is recurring debt, consider addressing it holistically.'

4. The executor prompt includes relevant debt items as watch items: 'Don't make these worse.'

5. A CLI command to manage debt: megaplan debt list, megaplan debt add, megaplan debt resolve. Resolving removes it and records which plan fixed it.

6. When debt items accumulate in the same subsystem (e.g. 3+ items tagged 'timeout-recovery'), surface this clustering to the critiquer as a signal to recommend redesign rather than another point fix.

User notes and answers:
- Critical design requirement: debt entries must be structured and deduplicated. Each entry needs: a subsystem tag (e.g. 'timeout-recovery', 'execute-paths', 'observation'), a normalized concern string, and the flag IDs that contributed to it. Before adding, the system must check existing entries by subsystem tag and semantic similarity of the concern — if a match exists, increment its occurrence count and append the new flag ID rather than creating a duplicate. The critique prompt should show debt entries grouped by subsystem so the critiquer sees clustering naturally. The gate should auto-escalate when a subsystem has 3+ debt entries: 'This subsystem has been flagged N times across M plans — recommend holistic redesign rather than another point fix.' The add operation should be available both as a CLI command (megaplan debt add) and as an automatic side-effect when a gate force-proceeds past a flag.

Missing one will be a runtime TypeError. They can differ. All debt I/O uses root. Double-check every load/save call.\n\n**Backward compatibility**: debt.json won't exist for plans started before this feature. \"flag_id\": flag[\"id\"],\n+                \"debt_id\": match[\"id\"],\n+                \"subsystem\": subsystem,\n+                \"concern\": flag[\"concern\"],\n+ subsystem in escalated_lookup:\n+            overlapping_escalated_subsystems.add(subsystem)\n \n     result: GateSignals = {\n         \"robustness\": robustness,\n         \"signals\": {\n             \"iteration\": iteration,\n@@\n             \"plan_delta_from_previous\": plan_delta,\n             \"recurring_critiques\": recurring,\n             \"scope_creep_flags\": [flag[\"id\"] for flag in open_scope_creep],\n             \"loop_summary\": loop_summary,\n+            \"debt_overlaps\": debt_overlaps,\n+            \"escalated_debt_subsystems\": [\n+                {\n+                    \"subsystem\": subsystem,\n+                    \"total_occurrences\": escalated_lookup[subsystem],\n+                }\n+                for subsystem in sorted(overlapping_escalated_subsystems)\n+            ],\n         },\n         \"warnings\": [],\n     }\n@@\n     if iteration >= 12:\n         result[\"warnings\"].append(\n             f\"Iteration {iteration}: hard iteration limit reached. Escalation is likely warranted.\"\n         )\n+    for subsystem in sorted(overlapping_escalated_subsystems):\n+        result[\"warnings\"].append(\n+            \"Recurring debt detected in subsystem \"\n+            f\"'{subsystem}' (total occurrences: {escalated_lookup[subsystem]}). \"\n+            \"Recommend holistic redesign rather than another point fix.\"\n+        )\n     return result\n*** End Patch\n"
}

> DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Persistent tech debt registry for megaplan

## Problem
The same subsystem gets flagged and point-fixed across multiple plans without being properly redesigned. In this session, timeout recovery was flagged in 4 separate plans. Each plan did a point fix. The critiquer had no way to know 'this has been flagged before — stop patching and redesign it.'

## Solution
A .megaplan/debt.json registry that persists across plans:

1. When a critique flag is accepted as a known limitation (force-proceed, or gate says 'accepted tradeoff'), it gets added to debt.json with the flag ID, concern, which plan accepted it, and when.

2. The critique prompt includes the debt registry as context: 'These are known accepted limitations — don't re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse.'

3. The gate prompt includes it too: if the current plan's flags overlap with existing debt, the gate can note 'this is recurring debt, consider addressing it holistically.'

4. The executor prompt includes relevant debt items as watch items: 'Don't make these worse.'

5. A CLI command to manage debt: megaplan debt list, megaplan debt add, megaplan debt resolve. Resolving removes it and records which plan fixed it.

6. When debt items accumulate in the same subsystem (e.g. 3+ items tagged 'timeout-recovery'), surface this clustering to the critiquer as a signal to recommend redesign rather than another point fix.

User notes and answers:
- Critical design requirement: debt entries must be structured and deduplicated. Each entry needs: a subsystem tag (e.g. 'timeout-recovery', 'execute-paths', 'observation'), a normalized concern string, and the flag IDs that contributed to it. Before adding, the system must check existing entries by subsystem tag and semantic similarity of the concern — if a match exists, increment its occurrence count and append the new flag ID rather than creating a duplicate. The critique prompt should show debt entries grouped by subsystem so the critiquer sees clustering naturally. The gate should auto-escalate when a subsystem has 3+ debt entries: 'This subsystem has been flagged N times across M plans — recommend holistic redesign rather than another point fix.' The add operation should be available both as a CLI command (megaplan debt add) and as an automatic side-effect when a gate force-proceeds past a flag.

Missing one will be a runtime TypeError. They can differ. All debt I/O uses root. Double-check every load/save call.\n\n**Backward compatibility**: debt.json won't exist for plans started before this feature. Every function that loads debt must handle the missing-file case gracefully (return empty registry). build_gate_signals in T13 must not crash when debt.json is absent.\n\n**Test strategy**: Run tests/test_core.py after T3. Run tests/test_megaplan.py after T6 and T9. Run full suite after T14. > DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Persistent tech debt registry for megaplan

## Problem
The same subsystem gets flagged and point-fixed across multiple plans without being properly redesigned. In this session, timeout recovery was flagged in 4 separate plans. Each plan did a point fix. The critiquer had no way to know 'this has been flagged before — stop patching and redesign it.'

## Solution
A .megaplan/debt.json registry that persists across plans:

1. When a critique flag is accepted as a known limitation (force-proceed, or gate says 'accepted tradeoff'), it gets added to debt.json with the flag ID, concern, which plan accepted it, and when.

2. The critique prompt includes the debt registry as context: 'These are known accepted limitations — don't re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse.'

3. The gate prompt includes it too: if the current plan's flags overlap with existing debt, the gate can note 'this is recurring debt, consider addressing it holistically.'

4. The executor prompt includes relevant debt items as watch items: 'Don't make these worse.'

5. A CLI command to manage debt: megaplan debt list, megaplan debt add, megaplan debt resolve. Resolving removes it and records which plan fixed it.

6. When debt items accumulate in the same subsystem (e.g. 3+ items tagged 'timeout-recovery'), surface this clustering to the critiquer as a signal to recommend redesign rather than another point fix.

User notes and answers:
- Critical design requirement: debt entries must be structured and deduplicated. Each entry needs: a subsystem tag (e.g. 'timeout-recovery', 'execute-paths', 'observation'), a normalized concern string, and the flag IDs that contributed to it. Before adding, the system must check existing entries by subsystem tag and semantic similarity of the concern — if a match exists, increment its occurrence count and append the new flag ID rather than creating a duplicate. The critique prompt should show debt entries grouped by subsystem so the critiquer sees clustering naturally. The gate should auto-escalate when a subsystem has 3+ debt entries: 'This subsystem has been flagged N times across M plans — recommend holistic redesign rather than another point fix.' The add operation should be available both as a CLI command (megaplan debt add) and as an automatic side-effect when a gate force-proceeds past a flag.

They can differ. All debt I/O uses root. Double-check every load/save call.\n\n**Backward compatibility**: debt.json won't exist for plans started before this feature. > DEVELOPER

Execute the approved plan in the repository.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan

        Idea:
Persistent tech debt registry for megaplan

## Problem
The same subsystem gets flagged and point-fixed across multiple plans without being properly redesigned. In this session, timeout recovery was flagged in 4 separate plans. Each plan did a point fix. The critiquer had no way to know 'this has been flagged before — stop patching and redesign it.'

## Solution
A .megaplan/debt.json registry that persists across plans:

1. When a critique flag is accepted as a known limitation (force-proceed, or gate says 'accepted tradeoff'), it gets added to debt.json with the flag ID, concern, which plan accepted it, and when.

2. The critique prompt includes the debt registry as context: 'These are known accepted limitations — don't re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse.'

3. The gate prompt includes it too: if the current plan's flags overlap with existing debt, the gate can note 'this is recurring debt, consider addressing it holistically.'

4. The executor prompt includes relevant debt items as watch items: 'Don't make these worse.'

5. A CLI command to manage debt: megaplan debt list, megaplan debt add, megaplan debt resolve. Resolving removes it and records which plan fixed it.

6. When debt items accumulate in the same subsystem (e.g. 3+ items tagged 'timeout-recovery'), surface this clustering to the critiquer as a signal to recommend redesign rather than another point fix.

User notes and answers:
- Critical design requirement: debt entries must be structured and deduplicated. Each entry needs: a subsystem tag (e.g. 'timeout-recovery', 'execute-paths', 'observation'), a normalized concern string, and the flag IDs that contributed to it. Before adding, the system must check existing entries by subsystem tag and semantic similarity of the concern — if a match exists, increment its occurrence count and append the new flag ID rather than creating a duplicate. The critique prompt should show debt entries grouped by subsystem so the critiquer sees clustering naturally. The gate should auto-escalate when a subsystem has 3+ debt entries: 'This subsystem has been flagged N times across M plans — recommend holistic redesign rather than another point fix.' The add operation should be available both as a CLI command (megaplan debt add) and as an automatic side-effect when a gate force-proceeds past a flag.
